ParlaMint-GR_2016-12-01-S1-commons.u3	Thank you. Madam Minister, you have the floor for three minutes.
ParlaMint-GR_2016-12-01-S1-commons.u4	Thank you very much, Mr President. My colleague, we have discussed this. You will allow me, Mr President, to read exactly what I have to say - and I may be getting away from my time - because it is a very technical issue and they must also know what is going on in order to have a full picture of what is going on and what is going on in this whole situation. The tourist port - Marina Messology, with a capacity of two hundred and fifty locations of port, was located at the request of the Port Fund of Itolokarnania. The majority of the company "MARINA MESALOGI S.A.", which participated in the Port Fund of Atalokarania at 5%, which concluded a contract with the Greek State on 3-11-2009, which was automatically solved on the grounds of non-submission of guarantee letters. Therefore, the use and management of the Messology tourist port, located in the port of Messology, returned to the port of Itolokarnania, which has the use and exploitation of the area. The Port Fund of Iatokarnania submitted a request on behalf of the company "MARINA MESALOGI S.A." to amend the location of the tourist port and the Interministerial Committee of Tourism Ports adopted the relevant Ministerial Decision to modify the location decision. Subsequently, the Ministry of Tourism, on the basis of the opinions of the Law Council of the State, had to reconstruct the Marina concession contract with the Port Fund of Istanbul, came in contact with the Fund. However, the last one for a very long time has not shown the slightest intention of taking over the tourist port. At the same time, a judicial dispute began between the two main shareholders of MARINA MESALOGI S.A. and following a series of judgments, the Monomele Court of Messology appointed MARINA MESALOGI S.A. provisional management of the company. The members of the company’s temporary management elected a new board of directors and with the new composition of MARINAS MESALOGI S.A., in which the port fund retained 5% of the shares. MARINA MEASOLOGI S.A. requested that it be re-introduced for port purposes. At its meeting on 13 July 2016, the relevant Committee on Tourist Ports gave a favourable opinion to the required change of location and continued the planned procedure in order to draw up and sign a joint Ministerial Decision of the Ministry of Environment and Energy and the Ministry of Tourism. The Ministry of Tourism has taken all the actions it had to take under law, and has no other competence over this marina. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-GR_2016-12-01-S1-commons.u98	Thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen, the draft law incorporates five European directives into Greek legislation. The three of them concern the question of the application of the principle of equality, one concerns market abuse and the other concerns the protection of the euro and other currencies against counterfeiting. These are new substantive regulations - incorporation is carried out by intervention in Greek legislation - which serve to incorporate directives and, respectively, abolish or reform existing provisions. They are a product of careful work which, among other things, was aimed at at least the provisions relating to the application of the principle of equality to draw up them as a coherent text, in some way, to codify them. We cannot talk about a code, but we are now talking about a single text that comes under the old 2005 law that was repealed by Law 3304 and, of course, includes incorporation of three different directives dealing with the same broader subject. The bill is divided into four parts. The first part concerns the three Directives relating to the principle of equality. The second relates to the directive on market abuse, the third to the Currency Protection Directive. The object of the latter part is essentially relevant to that of the first part and concerns the creation of a new national incident control mechanism which affects the principle of equality and which is recorded in the context of the operation of services of the repressive and police mechanism, i.e. the police, the port and the services of the Ministry of Justice, i.e. in particular the penitentiary services.
ParlaMint-GR_2016-12-01-S1-commons.u99	Please, ladies and gentlemen, be quiet. It doesn't sound like the speaker.
ParlaMint-GR_2016-12-01-S1-commons.u100	Thank you, Mr President. The first part of the draft law, therefore, concerns the principle of equality, and this is another step in the direction already indicated in previous reforms, such as the introduction of the cohabitation pact, the abolition of deplorable acts of males - that is, the abolition of Article 347 of the Criminal Code - and the strengthening of anti-racist legislation. This promoted fortification of the principle of equality coincided, as is well known, with the reports by President Obama at the beginning of his recent visit to our country. It is thus complemented and generally reformed - in fact, some provisions are abolished - Law 3304/2005, which had initially incorporated the two Directives of 2000, but which involved some weaknesses which, on the European side, were highlighted and even by a letter from the European Commission. Of course, a new directive is also incorporated, Directive 2014/54/EU, with similar content. The two of the three Directives concern the possibility of discrimination, i.e. unequal treatment, in the context of the labour market. Arrangements have as a more specific objective the introduction of new definitions and new forms of discrimination which are more detailed than previous ones, in order to avoid ambiguities and possible legal frictions. A number of reasons for discrimination are added, such as chronic illness, family status and social status. In other words, discrimination is excluded, even for reasons relating to these criteria. In some cases we have a change in terminology from sexual orientation, sexual orientation, which is a broader concept. Of course, they are added as grounds for discrimination and the identity and characteristics of the sex. Moreover, the term'social status' - and this is clarified - can now be set within the regulatory framework and cases such as the discrimination of people simply because they are detained or dependent on drugs. It is also interesting that this further regulation also concerns cases of discrimination based on legitimate characteristics, as well as the very interesting case of indirect discrimination between citizens under the cover of certain concepts that appear to be, in the first instance, vague and neutral. As far as the labour sector is concerned, it extends to fields of both private and public law and on equal access and development in professional hierarchy, as well as to activities of apprenticeship, vocational guidance and vocational training. Some exceptions are, of course, foreseen. They are the ones which correspond to the provisions of Article 45 of the Treaty on European Union and Article 4 of Law 3528 concerning the specific obstacles to appointment to public administration on grounds of nationality, which have so far been the case. There is also provision for an application for treatment and for hierarchical recourse to the Code of Administrative Procedure. A new framework for social dialogue is being structured. The characteristic is that it is defined as a single monitoring body and the promotion of the principle of equality and its implementation. This means that the Equal Treatment Committee, which was set up and which was inactive, is being repealed. It was completely ineffective. This is one of the main reasons why we need new regulations integrating these directives. Of course, there are no other regulations which are more favourable to the principle of equality. The second part of the legislation concerns arrangements for dealing with market abuse and for the responsibilities of the Securities and Market Commission, with the incorporation of Directive 2014/57/EU. This is a chapter which refers to financial instruments and which, moreover, defines in order to ensure that there is no legal uncertainty next or indeed a problem of interpretation. It is laid down that the relevant behaviours and means covered by protection relate to both auctions and the relevant types, the relevant forms of electronic auctioning. There are, of course, some exceptions. However, the arrangement covers all relevant behaviours, as it provides for any financial instrument that falls under trade, commercial behaviour. The legislation includes definitions of authentic, precisely to avoid interpretative problems, such as the definition of the concept of an investment services undertaking, direct delivery contracts and other relevant arrangements. Operations such as the setting-up of a group on financial transactions using privileged information shall also be criminalised. Now, what do these directives deal with? The bill, ladies and gentlemen, is divided into four parts. In the first three parts we have the incorporation of three directives, codified on the principle of equal treatment, that is, the provisions apply to the public and private sectors, including access to work, recruitment conditions, access to and training. It also concerns remuneration, dismissal, security, work and equal treatment regardless of race, irrespective of colours of national or ethnic origin, universal pay, applicable in so far as it concerns social protection, social security, health care, social benefits, education and access, supply and supply of goods and services. It has been pointed out that this does not include cases of sexual orientation, gender identity. I must tell you that in the bill, which comes from the Ministry of Justice, we in fact incorporate three Union directives into our institutional arsenal, broadening their content, in order to align ourselves with what is in place in Europe for the respective categories and the relevant issues to which they relate, in the legislative areas. I would therefore like to say that in the first part the principle of equal treatment is a very important directive, protecting all citizens in the workplace and in the workplace, and that is very important. I have, of course, pointed out that there is one issue and that we have seen these in the committee, since the debate is unified, that retroactive terms and provisions contained in individual and collective agreements, business regulations and statutes which are contrary to the principle of equal treatment are abolished and annulled in Article 8. In other words, ladies and gentlemen, a term retroactively, which was jointly defined by the Contracting Parties, and which may, if the Contracting Parties were aware of it, do not, i.e. sign the new provisions, be abolished. And I said there should be a transitional time for it to heal. As far as the first part is concerned, which is a purely equal principle of treatment in the workplace and employment. In the second part we are dealing with market abuse. I must tell you that too much has changed in the financial landscape, in the market and in technology. This was, therefore, only possible in order to come to the incorporation in Greek legislation in 2005 with Law 3440, the 2003 directive, which had updated the European Union legal framework in such a way as to meet the current needs. The Rules of Procedure therefore reconstitute and supplement provisions and prohibitions and preventive measures are provided for. In the second part of this draft law, therefore, all necessary incorporations are made. Given that Mr Paralympicopoulos, who referred to them before me, has referred to the economy of the debate and time, to get into the issues of the amendments, I would like to say that he is, in fact, protected and regarded as a criminal act of any kind, providing misleading or false indications about the financial instrument, harmonising the Greek legislation with the provisions of the European Union Regulation on market abuse, the Securities and Market Commission is designated as a competent authority to safeguard the implementation and support the provisions of Regulation 596/2014 on specific market abuse. I must also tell you that administrative sanctions are also provided for, apart from the criminality of behaviour and the administrative measures that the Securities and Market Committee may impose when the provisions of this Regulation are violated. These depend on the amount of profits or losses avoided by the unlawful transaction and the fact that the infringement was made by a legal or natural person. These market abuses exist and you will see - and I imagine you have studied them - in the second part of this draft law. In the third part we have the protection of the euro and the other currencies from two causes. One reason is counterfeiting, and the second reason is the citadelism. As the counterfeiting of the euro creates serious financial damage and the euro is a goal of organised crime - which is organised crime, I must confess, it is very effective in forgery - the current criminal provisions are stricter, as they obviously do not prevent this crime from being committed. In the sense, therefore, this third part is spent on it. However, the directive also provides for the transmission of coins seized at the National Euro Coin Analysis Centre for analysis, detection and detection of further counterfeiting. In any event, the third part concerns only the protection of the euro and the other currencies. The fourth part, which has "birthed" many discussions in the committee throughout the range of our National Representation, concerns the creation of a national mechanism where, according to the bill, the Citizens' Counsel is defined as a national mechanism for investigating arbitrary incidents. The Citizens' Counselor I think we all trust him and have a good witness to him in order to fulfil his purposes, since he has been motivated and, since - I have made many reservations - he has this staffing capacity, which will allow him to manage arbitrary cases in the Security Councils and in detention stores. Here I have to tell you this. Of course, I've been placed and there's all in the Minutes. There are, of course, colleagues who are first-class, but those who are formerly remember very well that in 2011 we had legislated on Law 3938, which, ladies and gentlemen, provided for the setting-up of a committee to examine complaints in this respect. ParlaMint-GR_2016-12-01-S1-commons.u219	Thank you, Mr President. Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, the legislation in question is divided - as we have said and repeat - into four parts. As I said in my introduction to the committee, reading the explanatory memorandum to this law, one can observe that the same same cohabitation pact as those provided for in Article 1367 of the Civil Code, political and religious marriage, is fully equal to all rights and obligations arising from them. That is to say, the road opens up to the recognition of the cohabitation agreement between couples in employment, insurance, pensions, hereditary and tax matters, which have so far been excluded from the existing legal regime. At this point I would like to stress again that our objections relate only to the coexistence pact for homosexual couples. It does not concern what has recently been written, unfortunately, by the newspaper "SYRIZA", after having put it with SYRIZA, publishing the poll that presented the difference with the New Democracy - and I personally have never accepted and will not accept it, because I know that SYRIZA is very popularly - and then put it with the ANELs, saying that we do not respect human rights, since we reject everything in terms of races, ethnics, colours, religions against others, etc. That's not happening. We respect human rights and as far as we can from this position we will support them. Of course, ladies and gentlemen, on this point I should like to explain my concerns and ask specific questions, which were raised in detail in this draft law. I cannot, frankly, understand the necessity of this particular piece of legislation. In 2005, at the Government of Kostas Karamanlis, the New Republic incorporated two Directives into national law, Directive 43/2000 and Directive 78/2000, with the vote of No 3304/2005. So this law was created, which is now being replaced by another law, in this piece of legislation, which we are debating today and which we will shortly be called upon to vote on. The texts of the Directives were incorporated into national law and were law of the State at that time. In the same law, Articles 19 and 21 provided for those institutions which were responsible for controlling possible infringements of the law. There was, then, a complete and complete legislative function, a legislative function for which the New Democracy is currently not making any reference. It comes, of course, to revise it, by reforming the law at the time, without reacting at all. By means of verbal interventions on this directive, it is differentiated and promoted by this piece of legislation, abolishing an entire law, in view of its anachronistic nature. I ask the colleagues in the New Democracy. Was the law that you passed in 2005 anachronistic on the basis of the incorporation of directives into Greek law? I am speaking on behalf of the Group of the I would like to say, among other things, that national law is being adapted to European Union law on conditions. First of all, the text of the directive is adopted. In other words, if the directive does not provide any margin of discretion or discretion to the Member States, the wording of the directive is followed precisely. This is where it changes. It changes and nobody reacts. Of course, if it can and will be changed, the directive includes 'Member States'. This is not the case in these directives. But the new institutionalisation is based on an interpretative, in our view, abuse. One element that might have contributed to rethinking the n. 3304/2005 would be the official record of cases of discrimination at work due to sexual orientation or chronic diseases or social status. Has there been a record of such complaints? If yes, how many after 2005 was the law, which protected from discrimination? Is the number enough to enforce the abolition of the law at the time, of Law 3304/2005, and to be replaced, reformed with the new one we are called upon to vote on? Where was this great truth dictated from? I will answer you in my opinion. As is apparent from the third paragraph of the special party's explanatory statement, it was dictated by the case-law of the Court of Justice of the European Union and by the legislation of other Member States, such as England and France. Of course the candidate now President of France, Fillon, claims to abolish the cohabitation agreement. Judicial law, however, like the legal rules of other States, does not form national law. Nor can a recommendation from the Committee of Ministers of the Council of Europe CM/Rec (2010)5, since it is in itself a lower legal text. What does that text really mean? It says that, among other things, the CM/Rec Recommendation, as I told you, was adopted by the Member States to combat discrimination on the grounds of sexual orientation on 31-3-2010. Paragraph 29 of the recommendation is the one that the national legislature adopts in order to support, support and strengthen this legislation. In paragraph 29, it allows national legislation to recognise the Cohabitation Pact and enable States to provide means for resolving practical problems of social reality or allowing individual childbirth. Therefore, individual childbirth does not prevent members of a cohabitation agreement from making it, because in the light of sexual orientation everything is covered. I do not know whether the scope of equality can create real equality in everything, since we are equating everything or creating inequality, by achieving the opposite of what, in fact, we believe and pursue. Of course, as I have said before, this recommendation is only a lower legal text than that of the directives. It does not, therefore, interfere, but it leaves the power of the national legislator. Our national legislator takes this into account, in order to do what I have said to you, to incorporate his country's law into the recommendation, if this is not contrary to fundamental rule of state law. Is it contrary to the fundamental rules of state law, ladies and gentlemen? What is the source of law on which this new legislation is based? It is certainly not the Constitution of our country, it is certainly not the European Convention on Human Rights, which, as a supreme European legal text, could be a legal basis. Let us see what the European Convention on Human Rights says in Article 12. It refers to the right to conclude a marriage. He supports and respects marriage. So does our Constitution. He's not talking about sexual orientation anywhere. "If it is completed by marriage, the man and woman have the right to meet at a marriage and to establish a family according to those who do so by virtue of national law." On the contrary, in other words, the European Convention on Human Rights has not been revised, it protects the right to a marriage between men and women in Article 12. The new incorporation with abusive additions and replacements is therefore a legislative initiative underpinning its scope for family couples. If that is not the case, how is the replacement of the term 'genuine orientation' with that of'sexual orientation', when sexual orientation and sexual freedom are related to the reproduction of species and protect human dignity, that is, the way in which one should live as a self-conscious and conscientious being, as a subject whose thought and function is delimited by good habits? Divorcely, sexual orientation refers to freedom of relations and the protection of human value as individual internal function and the inviolability of self-determination. However, this freedom as a right, as all those protected by Articles 5 and 25 of the Constitution, should also be subject to demarcations. It's all delimited. Nothing is unrestricted and should not be. So, in the demarcations, they must be subject to - something that the highest legal text itself provides, that is, our Constitution - the rights it describes. The wide-ranging authors of this project can imagine what will happen to a young child's psychology, if the first grade teacher, with her right to self-determination, makes a gender change and the next school year appear before them as a teacher? Does he have the right to do it? He's got it. Can a transsexual be a tutor? I don't know, I'm asking. Can a gay guy be a babysitter? I don't know, I'm asking.

ParlaMint-GR_2016-12-01-S1-commons.u260	We have introduced two amendments. The first relates to the Scientific Committee, which needs to be done, so that we can establish a five-member advisory committee with an advisory role responsible for the training of the tour operator. The existence of this committee, which is composed of teachers - members of PPC, schools and departments related to the education of tour guides, such as philosophical schools, history and archaeology, literature, architecture and schools of social and humanitarian studies, is necessary in order to provide the necessary scientific guidance on the education of the tour guide, which is provided, on the one hand, through the tour schools and, on the other, through the rapid training programmes. Since we are aiming to reopen the Tours School, this specific scientific committee is absolutely essential to present proposals, to formulate the curriculum, to define the skills of the teachers to be recruited, depending on the courses taught and the proposals being made in respect of the training courses concerned, thereby ensuring the required high level of training provided through the tour schools, education. There was an eleven-member committee, which had never operated and was abolished, and after consulting the bodies we bring this five-member committee in order to be able to function. The second amendment concerns a regulation to ensure that there is no conflict between the tourist buses and the transport work that they are doing and the CBELs. It's the conspiracy of the Ministry of Transport, i.e. Minister Spirtzi and mine. Law 711/1977 regulates the operation of special tourist buses and the transport operation carried out exclusively by special public tourist buses. This dismantling provision had introduced an exception to this general arrangement, so that the CBS would also make one-day school tours subject to conditions. As a result, these CBS are able, despite the fact that they are not specially tourist buses, to carry the students when they were needed and, I repeat, on the same day, without a night's stay, to be able to do, in exceptional cases, part of the work that the legislator has reserved for special tourist buses. By this exception, it essentially invaded the professional activity of the special tourist buses, without any reason sufficient, of another business category, the CBS, which have the task of conducting a regular transport operation. The practical application of the exemption introduced during the years during which it took place actually highlighted the abolished provision as a cause of terrible tensions between the CBES and the tourist buses. In order to solve this problem, therefore, the legislator brought Law No 2963/2001, which laid down all other forms of transport by the CBS as possible, as long as they set up independent transport undertakings for this project, with a separate installation and a different LNG. Therefore, the proposed abolition seeks to remove this lasting challenge and restore this clear demarcation of the scope of business on the one hand of the CBCs and of the special tourist buses on the other. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-GR_2016-12-01-S1-commons.u261	Thank you. Mr. Haritsis has the floor.
ParlaMint-GR_2016-12-01-S1-commons.u262	Thank you, Mr President. We, as the Ministry of Economy, are introducing an amendment, which first of all relates to four provisions - amendments to Law4314/2014. The first provision, the addition of a new Article 27a, relates to the simplification of procedures for the signature of the public investment programme by reducing the number of signatures required by the administration for the completion of the relevant payments, as well as to the homogenisation of the procedures, following the new role and functioning of the Ministries-General for Finance. An important element in this arrangement is the dismantling of signature payments by the political leadership for specific amounts. So that these payments can proceed only with the signatures of the administration. The second amendment relates to Article 47 of the same law and relates to the regulation of the payment of innovation projects raters made by the Secretariat-General for Research and Technology. In the third regulation, on Article 51 of the same law, under Law No.4314, we are restored an ambiguity that existed in the current context, in relation to the role of the Minister of Economy, in terms of concluding agreements. This is also relevant to the debate we had yesterday on agreements which the Ministry of Economy is implementing with international financial institutions - such as the European Investment Fund - in the next few months. It is therefore clear that the role of the Minister of Economy in this process of concluding agreements is being played, so that many authorisations, an excessive number of administrative acts, are not required which delay the implementation and signing of these agreements. With the fourth regulation, again in the same law, Law No.4314, following the establishment of the Ministry of Digital Policy, the final structure of the Ministry of Infrastructure should be transferred to the newly established Ministry. However, as we are in the process of completing and submitting to the European Commission the 2007-2013 closure report and since this final structure is responsible for completing the programs of the "Digital Convergence" Operational Programme, this service is transferred to the Ministry of Economy for the smooth completion of the programs by 31 March 2017 and then, as from 1 April 2017, it is normally transferred to the Ministry of Digital Policy, as required by the relevant law. The fifth provision is an autonomous provision and relates to the smooth completion of projects, in which implementing entities are municipal water and sewerage companies and relates to projects, which had been committed in 2014, in the process of resolving the NRP programmes for the period 2007-2013. Under the current scheme, we have the possibility of financing these projects only up to 35% of their total budget and this regulation allows for increased funding from the National Public Investment Programme up to 100%. Therefore, we will be able to proceed with national resources in the smooth completion of projects that had stopped in previous years from being financed by the NRP. And the sixth and last rules contained in this amendment concern L.3419/2005 and it is about the liquidation of state registers, on the basis of the actual business closure time, so that the clearance of GFCM registers can proceed in a way consistent with the actual operating conditions and closure of business and not many times the delays observed in the administration, in relation to the updating of business closure systems. Thank you, Mr President.

ParlaMint-GR_2016-12-01-S1-commons.u301	It is known that the unequal treatment of persons with characteristics, associated with race, ethnic origin, colour, natural weakness or lag, sexual orientation, family status, social status and other features is extremely widespread, especially today in times of crisis, with society on the grounds of poverty, unemployment and entry into the country of migrants and refugees. Unequal treatment concerns important areas of economic and social life and activity, particularly employment and work. There is therefore a need to further shield the relevant legislation and, above all, to implement it more effectively. That is the purpose of this draft law, namely, that of that first part. The first part of the draft law in question concerns the implementation of equal treatment, in line with the legislative framework of Directive 2043 on the application of equal treatment of persons irrespective of racial or ethnic origin, Directive 2078 on the establishment of a general framework for equal treatment in employment and work and Directive 2014/54 on measures to facilitate the exercise of workers. The aim of the legislature is to make a legal contribution to the implementation of the principle of equal treatment for all, regardless of racial or ethnic origin, religious or other beliefs, disability, sexual orientation, etc., so that there is legal certainty. In addition to harmonising these Community directives, this draft law seeks the synergy of all the relevant institutions and mechanisms of the State, as well as their cooperation with collectives and social partners, in order to combat in practice the deficit of equal treatment. In particular, the draft law aims to encourage changes, by establishing a set of provisions that ensure the effective implementation of the principle of equal treatment, by defining the independent authority of the Citizens' Co-legislator, as a body to monitor and promote the application of the principle of equal treatment in the public sector, the wider public sector and the private sector. Unequal treatment is still a problem at European level today. It is therefore necessary, on the one hand, to harmonise national legislation with European directives and, on the other, to establish a coherent institutional framework in this area and more effective auditing institutions in order to avoid discrimination. Greece belongs to those countries in Europe which, prior to the adoption of the 2000 Directives, had no special legislation to ensure the principles of equal treatment and non-discrimination. Law 3304/2005, which incorporated the relevant provisions of the two Directives, is reformed by this bill, because weaknesses had been identified. In this regard, new criteria are introduced: The race, colour, ethnic origin and genealogical payments. They are also added as grounds for discrimination: Chronic illness, family status, social status. Replace the anachronistic term ‘generative orientation’ with the term ‘sexual orientation’ and, in addition, expressly mention the gender identity and gender characteristics as a basis for distinction. In addition, the concept of disability, as a reason for discrimination, is supplemented by the term 'chronic illness', with a view to extending the attempted standard of protection for equal access and participation in the social protection of disabled persons. Discrimination on grounds of legitimate characteristics is also provided, i.e. a person being treated less favourably, because it is assumed that he has specific characteristics, which are a ground for discrimination. The second chapter regulates the scope of equal treatment or equal opportunities in the field of employment and employment, as well as in vocational education, both in the private and public sectors. Guarantees for equal access to professional hierarchy and positive action and measures are being introduced. Exemptions are provided for in a narrow and restrictive manner under Article 45 of the Treaty on European Union and the Staff Code and the Code of Municipal and Community Staff. Achieving equal treatment in employment is therefore of paramount importance, not only because it is a legal obligation, but also because it brings significant advantages to workers and businesses. The positive working environment gives workers satisfaction, favours increased efficiency and strengthens the productivity of enterprises. I will not go into the second chapter because time is running. I'll just refer to the latter...

ParlaMint-GR_2016-12-01-S1-commons.u370	Because of a short time, I will only refer to the last part of the bill, which concerns the establishment of the national mechanism for investigating arbitrary incidents. I shall try to prove why, in our view, voting on these articles is now an urgent necessity. In recent years, regrettably, acts of violence have been committed, even individually, by officials from the Security Councils and by penitentiary officers. The country has even been convicted before the European Court of Human Rights of infringements of Article 2 concerning the right to life and Article 3 prohibiting torture and any other inhuman and degrading treatment or punishment. These articles, of course, are included in the European Convention on Human Rights, which all European citizens and European governments should respect without question. It has been found that in the past there has been an inertia and a relaxation in the control of such acts, which have been carried out by institutions which were part of the same administrative body as those involved. The fact that the disciplinary investigation, the so-called SAD, usually did not result in concrete results, should only cause the lawmakers and ordinary citizens of the country. Every year, there was this unfinishedness to show a particular study of disciplinary bodies to finally relieve all responsibilities and the category of men of the Security Councils in the individual cases identified and controlled. Ladies and gentlemen, we must understand and realise that when controls are and are controlled and subject to the same institution, there is little effectiveness in investigating cases. When, on the other hand, the control authority is at some distance and is independent, the effectiveness of this control is guaranteed to a much greater extent. This has shown, again, the convictions of the European Court of Justice, and this itself has shown that the statistical valuation, according to which it did not appear to be adequate, was not sufficient, as was at least the case with the internal affairs services of the Security Councils. The need for an independent and effective mechanism for investigating arbitrary incidents has also highlighted many national and international human rights protection bodies. For these reasons, the fourth part of this draft law is replaced by Article 1 of Law 3938/2011, which established the Office to deal with arbitrary cases in order to assess complaints of the Security Councils against citizens, and the relevant competence is now delegated to the Citizens' Ombudsman, a constitutionally-established independent authority, which undertakes to investigate complaints on substance in order to ensure, as I have said, the independent, reliable and effective nature of this investigation. The involvement, if you wish, of the Citizens' Counsel, the investigation of cases rather by the Citizens' Counsel may begin on its own and is carried out, unlike what has been heard in this Chamber, in a strict timeframe. Where the Citizens' Counsel promotes a case to the disciplinary bodies of each service, these bodies shall examine it by priority and in turn inform the Citizens' Counsel, regarding the outcome of the examination, by forwarding copies of all the details of the file and by suspending the adoption of the decision until the conclusion of the Citizens' Counsel. Extremely important is the provision that any discrepancy in the decision of the disciplinary body from the conclusion of the Citizens' Counselor may be allowed only by specific and detailed reasons, further limiting the arbitrariness or non-exhaustive decision of the disciplinary bodies. Compared with the previous law, it should be stressed that the investigation of complaints now includes the illegal conduct that may have been committed by racist motive, and that the competence of the new national mechanism is extended beyond the Security Body and the employees of detention stores. Finally, an important element for the proper and effective functioning of the mechanism is the provision for publication of a relevant special report by the Citizens' Counselor, which is submitted each year to the President of the Parliament, is discussed in accordance with the Rules of Procedure of the Parliament and published in a special edition of the National Printing Office. In this report, the Citizens' Counsel sets out the whole work of the Authority, presents the most important cases and, of course, sets out proposals for improving public services as well as the necessary and necessary legislative arrangements. It is therefore only about mature works, which are currently being stopped, so that these works can be completed. Thank you.

Minister of Justice, ladies and gentlemen, it has been said internationally that we live in the 21st century, the century of human rights. Of course, taking into account and taking advantage of all the issues judged by the case law and by the Court of Justice of the European Union and the European Commission, the recommendations of the international organisations and the reports of the specialised bodies. The National Mechanism will no longer operate under the control or supervision of a Ministry. For reasons of transparency, operational independence and efficiency, it will operate within the framework of the Citizens' Counselor, which is an independent authority and has dealt with relevant issues for years, namely know-how. ParlaMint-GR_2016-12-01-S1-commons.u473	Ladies and gentlemen, the present draft law of the Ministry of Justice, Transparency and Human Rights incorporates four European Union directives, namely Articles of Regulation 596/2014 on market abuse, while providing for the establishment of the National Mechanism for Investigation of Accidents in Security Body, attempting to eliminate and also address in a more effective way discrimination based on sex, origin, religion, belief, illness, disability, age, family status, sexual orientation. [[At this point, the F Vice-President of Parliament, Mr GEORGIOS LAMPROYLIS, takes over the office of President.]] Three are the reasons for this draft law: firstly, to modernise the existing regulatory framework, secondly, to ensure fuller harmonisation with European Union legislation and thirdly, to implement the principle of equal treatment more effectively in practice. Basically, the aim of this draft law is to create a uniform, clear and perfect framework for how equal treatment will be applied on the basis of the case-law of the Court, the decisions I mentioned before and the European Union directives and reports - we would say - of the national bodies, which we have in our hands. What's the new element in this bill? It is the responsibility to monitor and promote the principle of equal treatment conferred on the Citizens' Ombudsman, who is becoming a body responsible for the public and wider public sector, but also for the private sector, and this is an extremely important legislative intervention, given that the Citizens' Ombudsman is an independent authority, it is the Authority which is primarily responsible for human rights matters and has constitutional and legislative independence and autonomy over executive power, we would say, in an extensive area of competence, about effective force and the conduct of investigations, as well as resources to carry out these tasks. Also, this bill abolishes the Committee on Equal Treatment of the Ministry of Justice, which redefined the cooperation of the Citizens' Counsel with the Labour Inspectorate. Finally, the conditions are in place for an open social dialogue and reconnection with the civil society, given that we have a modernisation and an increase in the effectiveness of the regulatory framework for social dialogue. There is also provision for extending the reasons for equal treatment by contrasting the identification of social prejudices, and the negative ones, we would say, stereotypes, which are the reasons for this unequal treatment and intolerance, which contribute to the social stigmatisation of discrimination in our country. Basically, we would say that it is a multi-brand plan by which the government is attempting to strengthen its legislative framework in the field of discrimination, particularly in the field of labour, the production of goods and services, education, tax facilities, social protection, and even access to housing. So, through this bill, discrimination, on the basis of the above-mentioned characteristics and elements of the identity of the citizens, is punished by criminal and financial sanctions. Also, to deal with all kinds of discrimination, even for cases of arbitrary civil servants, the Citizens' Ombudsman plays a key role, which takes over all the enlargement and combats such phenomena. The measures therefore open up the way for greater protection in sensitive areas, such as access and conditions at work, goods and services to education, and raise protection shields for thousands of people who currently face problems because of race, religious or other beliefs, sexual orientation, etc. The draft law also broadens the scope of protection, adding to this deficit of our legislative framework until now and to some other categories of non-discrimination, such as ethnic origin, chronic disease, family and social situation, sexual orientation, identity and gender characteristics. In practice they can be better protected and prevent any discrimination against persons and citizens who are currently on the margin. And these are conscientious, seropositive and seropositive, suffering and suffering from other diseases, former drug addicts and so on. It also protects their relatives, who, because of their relationship with them, may also be treated in the same way. In the field of non-discrimination, protective rules concern the conditions for access to employment, vocational training, remuneration, redundancies, health and safety, reintegration and reemployment after long periods of unemployment and workers' trade union participation. At the same time, they apply to matters relating to social protection and tax facilitation, for example taking into account tax treatment instead of nationality, also to education, access to supplies of goods, etc., which, by a Presidential Decree, can be extended to other sectors, as the Government considers necessary. They are recognised - and here we must note - essential rights to the partners in the cohabitation agreement, particularly in the workplace, where they can claim co-responsibility, family allowances and other issues. It is extremely important that, on the basis of this bill, social organisations, trade unions are allowed to represent the affected person in the courts, without being forced to pay insane payments. I also think the government is trying to deal with much more important issues of arbitrariness, i.e. abuses, tortures, etc., committed by police, ports, penitentiary officers - not by the majority, but we often have complaints on these issues - and shields human rights in this context in order to deal with any kind of arbitrary nature, for which our country has been convicted and forced to pay compensation. The single and clear legislative framework and parallel actions through education thus form the consciences of people, citizens, who spontaneously act in accordance with the law as if it had not been imposed. This is exactly what is coming to do with the bill being passed. It legislates against discrimination, defines the Independent Authority of the Citizens' Counsel, which has the guarantees of independence, experience and know-how to oversee and enforce the law. The standard of Article 21 of the Charter of Fundamental Rights of the European Union establishes a broader scope of equal treatment than that of Law 3304/2005. The reasons for non-discrimination and discrimination in the field of employment and employment and in trade in accordance with Article 11 of the draft law are redefined. Sanctions in the event of non-compliance with the principle of equal treatment, the possibility for the affected person to be represented by legal persons, organisations and associations with a simple authorisation, the reversal of the burden of proof - the opposition of the affected party must prove rather than the affected party - and what has happened, help to ensure that the provisions adopted do not remain a simple wishlist. In order to enable the Independent Authority of the Citizens' Counsel to meet its expanded powers as a monitoring body and to promote equal treatment, amendments are necessary in their statutes. I will bypass Part II and Part III of the bill and go to Part IV, which deals with the establishment of the national arbitrary investigation mechanism. Part IV of the bill is satisfied with a consistent demand for democratic citizens to create an observatory of the ways of action of the Security Councils, a mechanism to investigate acts of arbitraryness and an assessment of relevant complaints. Many have witnessed incidents of arbitrariness and general misconduct of class organs. The idea of creating the national mechanism is not new. There was before 2011. And 2011 was practically recognised when Article 1 of Law 3938/2011 established an arbitrary management office, which was not formed and never operated. However, even if this office were set up and operated, it could not produce the results for which it was set up, because it did not fulfil the guarantees of independence. And that's why? Because it was subject to the Minister for Citizen Protection, but also to why the Complaints Investigation Committee was responsible only for monitoring and, in the event of a positive crisis, for promoting the complaint for further investigation. A rule of law, however, must ensure that a complaint is investigated fairly and effectively. In this draft law, the investigation of the complaint concerning an arbitrariness of the Security Councils is undertaken by the Independent Authority of the Citizens' Counsel and the scope is extended, as it includes outside the three Security Councils - Police, Port and Firefighting - and workers in detention stores. The national investigative mechanism shall operate at the same time and complement the disciplinary and criminal investigation without substituting them. It is an additional measure of effective investigation and it is very positive that this mechanism is also on its own to investigate incidents that come to its knowledge of publications, news in the media or in any other way. In the case investigated by the mechanism, a finding, in a strictly defined timeframe, shall be drawn up after the disciplinary review. In the event of a discrepancy of the decision to disciplinary review with the content of the finding of the mechanism, specific, detailed reasons shall be required. In this way it is achieved, in addition to other things, and prevention of arbitrary acts. The arbitrary body knows that there is still an additional instrument for controlling its operations, except for the Ministry in which it serves, and this will act dissuasively. The mechanism of investigation, in the end, helps to ensure that the Security Body operates by performing the role that they have set to perform, that of protecting citizens and consolidating their sense of security, without infringing human rights. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2016-12-01-S1-commons.u527	So we proceed with the SYRIZA Parliamentary spokesman, Mr. Tsirka.
ParlaMint-GR_2016-12-01-S1-commons.u528	Minister, ladies and gentlemen, this bill contains substantial regulations with the incorporation of directives that reform the legislation in force so far with new provisions of the Ministry of Justice. It moves in the same progressive direction with a number of recent reforms that have taken place before, such as the introduction of the coexistence pact and the strengthening of anti-racist legislation. This legislation also confirms our commitment to the value of equality as a cultural and historical element of the country, an element of democracy. The legislation is divided into four parts: the first part concerns the implementation of the principle of equality and the main objective of the draft law is the creation of a clear legal framework for the application of the principle of equal treatment. New criteria for the recognition of discrimination are introduced by adding new terms, such as race, colour, ethnic origin, genealogical payments, and new grounds for possible discrimination are added to the list of possible discriminations, chronic disease, family status and social status. The oldest term ‘family orientation’ is replaced by ‘sexual orientation’, since that term relates more widely to human behaviour in love matters and, moreover, expressly refers to as grounds for discrimination and gender identity and gender characteristics. The competence to monitor and promote the implementation of anti-discrimination on grounds of race, colour, ethnic or ethnic origin, religious or other beliefs, chronic illness, age, family status, sexual orientation and gender identity in the field of employment and labour is conferred on the Citizens' Counselor. In this way, the committee provided for by the current legislation is abolished as part of the Ministry of Justice and ten posts are thus recommended in the Citizens' Counsel. The Citizens' Counsel now has essential tasks and plays an important role in this field. This transfer of powers to combat discrimination by the Committee on Equal Opportunities restores - it could be said - and the link with civil society in the context of a worthwhile reform of the democratic principles of non-discrimination against respect for diversity in Greek society. This choice is a guarantee for the greatest possible institutionalisation of supervision of the principle of equality. In fact, this is a very important and critical initiative, which, of course, has as an axis and harmonisation of Greek legislation with European legislation and the European acquis and gives answers to critical issues of armour - one would say - democratic values. The provisions of this bill also lead to an equilibrium of the cohabitation agreement with marriage, effectively recognizing the same rights in the workplace and, therefore, they can also claim co-service, family allowances and much more, which until now was impossible. In both the private and the public sectors protection regulations are introduced, concerning the prohibition of discrimination, vocational education and training, in matters of remuneration, redundancies, health and safety at work, reintegration and reemployment after periods of unemployment, as well as the trade union participation of workers. It is also foreseen that in cases where the employer is found to be unfavourable to the worker and vice versa, discrimination on the grounds of sex, religion and all the other things I have mentioned earlier, there is a six-month term of imprisonment of up to three years and a penalty of EUR 1 000 to 5 000 following a complaint to the Citizens' Ombudsman, who is given a mediation role in restoring the problem that has arisen, such as dismissal or deterioration. It is very important, therefore, that enlargement to the protection field, namely, that it is added to the current deficit legislative framework and other categories of non-discrimination. The second part of this draft law incorporates Directive 57/2014 in the Greek legal order, covering either a wide range of financial instruments listed, or an application for admission to trading in the regulated market, and also includes auction-related behaviour based on those rights, even in auctions that do not concern financial instruments. The same Party shall include criminal provisions that protect the proper functioning of the market from abuses in order not to affect the integrity of financial markets and public confidence in securities, derivative instruments and rating criteria. It is obvious that the importance of the organised market as a means of collecting such risk capital and developing public and private enterprises is crucial for the national economy and it is necessary to protect and regulate this field. For the first time criminalise the recommendation for financial transactions using privileged information, as well as the unlawful announcement of such privileged information. This is criminalisation of behaviours that affect the proper functioning of the capital market and the confidence of the investment public in this function. Obviously, the review of some of the articles of today's bill leads us to useful points on these issues, such as the fact that the objective of obtaining or disposing of such crimes is objectively necessary. A particularly distinguished malicious case of misuse of such privileged information is described when the perpetrator of the crimes concerned is a member of a criminal organisation within the meaning of Article 187 of the Criminal Code. Including, therefore, detailed and specific procedural arrangements, which facilitate the above progress of the procedure and transitional provisions. Furthermore, the main provisions of the repealed Article 18 of Law 3340/2015, which provides for the registration and archiving of all orders that give customers for training in financial instruments, even by telephone, are recast. Such recording shall be done in such a way as to ensure the reliability, accuracy and completeness of the recorded data. The third part of the bill incorporates in Greek legislation rules on counterfeiting, according to the provisions of the directive, which allow them to fall within the scope of criminal law and a number of other acts which echo modern technological developments and may affect the Euro or another currency. In the last part of the bill, an independent arbitrary Investigation Mechanism is established in the Security Corps and Prisoners, the National Branch Investigation Mechanism. It is well known that for such cases our country has been convicted by the European Court of Human Rights of violations of Articles 2 and 3 of the European Convention on Human Rights. It is a fact that, for the state we are striving to build, it is necessary, among other things, to take measures to ensure that such complaints are investigated fairly and that there is an appropriate institutional and legal framework so that there is no suspicion of cover or impunity. And it is a constant request for the Greek society to have no tolerance to such cases of arbitrary action. With this bill, therefore, the Citizens' Counsel, a constitutionally-established independent authority, undertakes to investigate complaints in order to ensure the independent, reliable and effective nature of this mechanism, so that society can again gain its confidence in the institutions. And it is important to say that this new National Success Investigation Mechanism does not replace judicial and disciplinary control, but will work in parallel and complementarity. Ladies and gentlemen, it is understandable to everyone that with the draft legislation under discussion we are establishing a strict framework that will eliminate the practices of marginalisation and isolation of our fellow citizens in the workplace and not only. Another cycle of denials and marginalisation is closed for a large part of our citizens and it is our duty to insist on the protection of rights by breaking stereotypes and dogmatisms. ParlaMint-GR_2016-12-01-S1-commons.u529	Mr. Chogas has the floor from SYRIZA.
ParlaMint-GR_2016-12-01-S1-commons.u530	Thank you, Mr President. Minister, ladies and gentlemen, today we are discussing a bill divided into four parts. In the first of these we are discussing the incorporation of three directives which will become internal law, number 43/2000, number 78/2000 and number 54/2014. It provides for the fair application of the principle of equal treatment of persons irrespective of their national or racial origin, the establishment of a general framework for equal treatment in employment and work and measures facilitating the exercise of the rights of workers in the context of the free movement of workers in the European Union. The second part concerns the incorporation of Regulation 596/2014 on market abuse and repealing Directive 66/2003 and the incorporation of Directive 57/2014 on criminal penalties for market abuse. The third part concerns the incorporation of Directive 62/2014 on the protection of the euro and other currencies against counterfeiting through criminal law. In the fourth section, reference is made to the establishment of the National Irregularity Investigation Mechanism in Security Corps and detainees. Ladies and gentlemen, the principle of equal treatment comes directly from the idea of justice itself and is an integral part of the legal order, the regulator of which is the Constitution itself. The safeguarding of the principle of equality with regard to the law, Article 4(1) of the Constitution, introduces the requirement for equal treatment of all organicly connected societies throughout the field of positive law. There is no jurisprudence in which there is no question of applying the principle of equality and that of equal treatment, even in sectors of private law in which the contractual freedom and autonomy of private will dominate. ‘ Thus, under the first part of this draft law, Law 3204/2005 which first established the framework for the application of the principle of equal treatment in the absence of racial or ethnic origin, religious or other beliefs, disability, age or sexual orientation is replaced for regulatory purposes, but also with a more complete harmonisation with Union law, and the provisions of the three European Directives, as mentioned, are to be codified, with a view to strengthening the existing institutional framework, combating discrimination and promoting the application of equal treatment and the creation of a single clear and sound legal framework, always having regard to the relevant case-law of the Court of Justice of the European Union, the positions of the European Commission, the recommendations of the international organisations and the reports of the international bodies. In the light of these considerations, the delegation of powers to monitor and promote the implementation of the principle of equal treatment, regardless of race, colour, ethnic origin, religious or other beliefs, disability or chronic illness, age, family or social status, sexual orientation, identity or gender characteristics, the Citizens' Ombudsman and indeed the public and the public sector and the private, was required, since such transfer of competence to the Citizens' Ombudsman was unquestionably a dominant legislative intervention, since the Citizens' Ombudsman has, as an independent authority, a wide scope of competence, regulated independence with wide legitimacy, elements establishing the certainty and effectiveness in the performance of his duties, as a supervised body of the principle of equal treatment and as guarantor of the application of the provisions of Directives 43, 78 and 54. In more detail, the first part of this Law consists of six chapters and twenty-four articles with continuous numbering. Articles 1 and 2 provide for this purpose and the concept of discrimination is analysed. In particular, Article 1 shall highlight the broadness of this draft law in relation to previous Law 3304, which finds scope for implementation with the incorporation of Directive 54/2014 on the facilitation of the freedom of movement of workers of the European Union, the declaration of the concept of ethnic origin, in accordance with Directive 43/2000, but also the inclusion of new grounds of discrimination in the field of employment and employment, such as the terms ‘chronic disease’ supplemented with that of disability, ‘family situation’ aimed at protecting the area of employment and work, the strong living links developed in the context of family life, regardless of the type of coexistence of the couple, the ‘social status’ as a ground for discrimination of social stigmatism due to the participation as a member of a particular social sub-group, such as former users or former prisoners, the ‘sexual orientation’ instead of the term ‘sexual identity’, which is other than a ‘unborn’. In Article 2, the concepts concerning the direct and indirect discrimination of Law 3304 and Directives 43 and 78 are enriched by the introduction of new definitions of discrimination, such as discrimination on grounds of relationship, discrimination on grounds of legitimate characteristics, and discrimination. In the second chapter, Articles 3 to 7, the title ‘Equal Treatment’ provides for cases of reciprocated differential treatment and the nationality and exemption clause of Law 3304/2005 are cleared. In the second part of this law, Directive 57/2014 of the European Parliament on criminal penalties in Articles 25 to 33 is incorporated into national legal order and the legislative framework on market abuse is complemented. Provision is made for measures to protect the money market, the stock exchange transactions of the Securities Committee, in order to prevent market manipulation, i.e. any action related either to the exploitation of confidential information or to the manipulation of the market. [[At this point the end of the speaking time of the honourable Member is ringing at the end of the bell.]] Wait a minute, Mr. President. According to Article 1(1) of Law 3094/2003, the Citizens' Ombudsman is an independent authority with a mission of mediation between citizens and public services. The mediation alone. Thus, in itself, the wording of that provision, which is maintained almost as it is in accordance with Article 18 of this draft law, is in principle outside its regulatory framework as the competence of the Citizens' Co-legislator as defined in the Law. Furthermore, the Citizens' Counsel is deprived of the power under the Constitution to deal with cases relating to the exercise of public service functions, which are part of the hard core of state powers, as in this case of Security Bodies and is de facto lacking in activity, as the Citizens' Counsel is structured and operates primarily with personnel not part of the hard core of state functions, as is apparent from Article 5 of Law 3054/2003. It is therefore not possible to deal with and judge public officials who do not fall within this service situation, and without the staff that manages this principle having the appropriate experience and knowledge. In this respect, it should be stated that public order and state security are the most public authority and expression of sovereignty that cannot be detached from the core of state power. Therefore, only by the State, and not by an independent authority, is the disciplinary authority and control over the acts and omissions of those public officials, either without the mediation or interference of another authority or authority organisationally not part of the institution of the person controlled or of the judicial authority. In addition to these findings, the individual provisions to be adopted give cause for concern both in their feasibility and in their legal correctness. In particular, the provision of the non-notification of the complainant’s name infringes a number of rights of the controlled public operator, and calls into question and, where appropriate, void the examination carried out. That's why? The non-publishment of the complainant's name, in addition to depriving him of the possibility of opposing and effective exercise of the right of defence by the person controlled, also runs counter to the principle of obvious action, a fundamental principle and value which runs our fairness and which carries out the principle of rule of law and transparency. Furthermore, I would like to point out that paragraph 4 of Article 56 of the draft law, whereby the competent disciplinary bodies suspend the adoption of a decision until a conclusion has been lodged by the Citizens' Counsel, circumvents the principle of self-sufficiency and the self-determination of the disciplinary procedure, a distinct and autonomous procedure which cannot be erroneous, dependent or ultimately limited by acts of non-judiciary or administrative authorities which are not part of the Security Body. Subsequently, when it comes to exercising the right of the person controlled to be effectively heard, a category of a certain nature should be established by the Citizens' Counselor, which, however, once it has been indicted or attributed, turns the Citizens' Counselor into an autonomous or, if you like, quasi-disciplinary body, a status which is not permitted by the Constitution and the law. In addition, the possibility of deviating from the conclusion of the Counsellor only by specific and detailed reasons makes the finding in substance unresolved and in any event an increased administrative finding which, if recognised as such, is reversed by the nature, content, extent and substance of the disciplinary review of the competent State bodies. In this respect, it should be noted that the right to self-infringement of the finding or even to submit a request for review and review is not granted. Irrespective of the above, the review of the case provided for in paragraphs 5 and 6 after the adoption of a judgment by the European Court of Human Rights effectively infringes the res judicata of the internal judicial or disciplinary bodies at the expense of the controlled person, a consequence which does not recognise international law, nor the European acquis. Such a recovery would, in substance, be unconscious and allowable only to act on the reintroduction of the victim and not on the re-checking or additional collapsing of the State institution which has exempted the charges before the national disciplinary courts. Consequently, the provisions of paragraph 6 of the draft law on the prosecution or the completion of disciplinary proceedings are incomprehensible to the Greek legal order and effectively call into question the national res judicata, making its position disciplinaryly audited and, indeed, for a procedure before the EDPS, in which it was not even a party and during which it was not heard. The provision for suspension of the deadline, an arrest of a global police originality, is exceptionally problematic and, if anything else, unconstitutional, as long as such suspension of the procedure and the deadline is not understood under our national law and our legal culture. In conclusion, it is not only the complainant who can rely on the submission of a complaint of minor causes or even because he considers that it cannot be subject to legal, state control by the police. There is also the public official who is controlled for his actions or omissions. In view of this, the proposed provisions introduce a unilateral concept and treatment, since they do not establish any insurance in favour of the investigated person, which makes the provisions incomplete and problematic, because, in view of the principles of transparency, non-confidence of the procedure, good administration and the need to protect the rights of the perpetrator, which impose as the legitimate interests of the person concerned are safeguarded and in order to facilitate the exercise of rights, the principle of fair trial, impartiality and the equality of the parties, make it compulsory for the competent audit authorities, in particular on disciplinary procedures which even the right to work for the persecuted person is at stake. Otherwise, protection should also be given to workers in the Security Councils who must be protected equally and facilitated in the exercise of their rights, which unfortunately do not exist in the draft legislation under discussion.

We did, therefore, proceed with a consolidation process, because we could not charge the national budget with immature projects for which the appropriations were not available. Where did this resolution process lead? It led to the fact that Greece is for the first time in absorption of resources, as much as Mr Constantinople denys this. We have the official information from the European Commission and I submit it for the Minutes. [[At this point the Deputy Minister for Economy and Development, Mr Alexandros Haritsis, presents for the minutes the above document, which is in the archives of the Secretariat of the Stenography and Practice Directorate of the Parliament.]] You will allow me to read the telegraph from the Permanent Greek Representation in Brussels, which says on 17 December 2015 that Greece is now in the first absorption position with the absorption rate of 97.1%. Our country also precedes the other Member States to absorb in proportion to the size of its financial dossier. I'm putting this in the Minutes too. [[At this point the Deputy Minister for Economy and Development, Mr Alexandros Haritsis, presents for the minutes the above document, which is in the archives of the Secretariat of the Stenography and Practice Directorate of the Parliament.]] A new telegraph on 22-1-2016 says that Greece remains firmly in the first position of absorption and even our country is well ahead of the implementation procedures of the NRP 2014-2020 as compared to the other Member States, as it has approved all twenty operational programmes of the new period and it is the only one that has succeeded in almost all its approved programmes to have completed the process of defining management, certification and control principles, which is prerequisite for the submission of requests for payments for the new programming period. I put this together with the tables from the European Commission that testify to my words. [[At this point the Deputy Minister for Economy and Development, Mr Alexandros Haritsis, presents for the minutes the above document, which is in the archives of the Secretariat of the Stenography and Practice Directorate of the Parliament.]] Ladies and gentlemen, we are of course aware that, on a parliamentary level, confrontation of arguments can also be avoided from the thresholds, and it can sometimes also take some excessive proportions, if you like, if you like.
